Studio A for TurnoutSwing, final, identity Marquise

Swing, final

The needle now pivots on a round hub set into the end of the left wall: the hub is tangent to the wall's top, bottom and end, and the wall is cut around it with a fine paper reveal, so the needle reads as hinged in the wall, not lying on it. At 512 px and up the socket is the true concentric cut, a 2 px reveal; at 256 and 128 the reveal is one pixel and the socket is hinted to whole pixels, so it reads as a clean paper column instead of grey half-tones. That hinting is for 1x screens: on a 2x screen the component draws the curved socket instead, so the hub never sits in a doubled staircase. Below 128 px, where the reveal would be a fraction of a pixel, the hub becomes a foot of whole pixels at the wall end with its outer corner rounded. The outer arc is thinner: 0.25 of the grid in full ink at 128 px and up, one device pixel of ink at 65 % below. Colours are Marquise: ink #111114, needle and afterglow #E0115F, paper #F2F3F3.

Construction

Hero-large 512 master on the 32 unit grid, at its native 16 px per unit. One datum (y 26, the wall-top line); arc, ring and afterglow about A (6, 26); the needle about its hub P (5.25, 26.75).

  1. aHub in its socket. The needle ends in a hub of r 0.75 inscribed in the wall end: tangent to the wall top y 26, the wall bottom y 27.5 and the jamb x 6, so wall plus hub are 4 long like the right wall. Its edges are straight and tangent to the hub, 1.5 wide there and 1.05 at the arc. The wall is cut 0.125 around it (2 px at 512), then drops square at x 4.5. At 256 (reveal 0.125) and 128 (reveal 0.25) the same socket is hinted: the wall ink ends on a whole pixel in every row, with one clear pixel column before the hub.
  2. bArc on the right wall. One outline with the right wall. Inner edge r 20 leaves the wall corner (26, 26) tangent to the jamb face; outer edge r 20.25 lands square on the wall top. Below 128 px the arc has its own solid 65 % colour and its foot runs on under the wall.
  3. cNeedle top on the arc. The axis runs from P to T (16.0848 8.7287), where the previous needle met the arc. The top is cut on r 20 and drawn on to r 20.125, under the arc.
  4. dArc top end. Cut on the radius x 6, from y 5.75 to 6.
  5. eRing ends. Band r 12 to 12.25 at 50 %, never heavier than the arc. Foot cut on the datum, top cut on x 6; it passes under the needle.
  6. fAfterglow edges. Floor on the datum, outer edge on r 20.125 under the arc, leaf side on the needle axis inside the needle. Nothing of it can show outside the needle, the arc or the datum.

Every joint, close up

The hero-large 512 rasterised at its native 512 px (16 px per unit), cropped and enlarged 3x without smoothing. Guides in blue: datum and x 6 dashed, arc inner edge solid, arc outer edge and ring dashed, hub dashed, socket solid, the needle axis dotted.

aHub in its socket

The needle ends in a round hub, r 0.75, tangent to the wall top, the wall bottom and the jamb. The wall end is cut concentric with it, 0.125 away: a 2 px paper reveal at 512, half the arc's weight.

bArc on the right wall

Right wall and arc are one ink outline. Inner edge r 20 leaves the wall corner tangent to the jamb face; outer edge lands square on the wall top.

cNeedle top on the arc

Cut on the arc's inner edge, r 20. Drawn on to r 20.125, half the band, hidden under the ink.

dArc top end

Cut on the radius x 6, y 5.75 to 6: square to both edges.

eRing foot

Band r 12 to 12.25 at 50 %, lighter than the arc. Foot cut on the datum.

eRing top end

Top cut on x 6. The ring passes under the needle.

fAfterglow edges

Shown at 4x opacity. Leaf side on the needle axis (inside the needle), outer edge on the arc centreline (under the arc), floor on the datum.

aHub at 1280

The same joint at 40 px per unit (a review size), 1:1.

aHinted socket at 256

The hero-large 256 at its native size, 6x: reveal 0.125 = 1 px, the wall ink ending on whole pixels (device columns 37, 36, 36, 35, 35, 35, 35, 35, 35, 36, 36, 36).

aHinted socket at 128

The hero-large 128 at its native size, 12x: reveal 0.25 = 1 px, wall ink to column 17 on the top and bottom rows, 16 between, then one paper column.

a128 on a 2x screen

At 128 CSS px and devicePixelRatio 2 the mark is 256 device px. The component swaps the 1x staircase, which doubled would show 2 px nubs, for the curved socket: reveal 0.25 = 2 device px, 6x.

aOne colour at 512

One colour and reverse have their own 256 and 512: the 128's reveal, 0.25, cut as a true curve concentric with the hub (2 px at 256, 4 px at 512), 3x.

eRing at 64

The hero's ring is 0.5 = 1 px at 35 %: both edges and both cut ends land on whole pixels, top end row 27, foot column 36, 8x.

aPixel foot at 64

The hero at its native 64, 8x: ink wall 5 px, raspberry foot 3 px with its corner pixel rounded, no reveal.

Sizes we ship

A mark is only ever drawn at the native size of a master, or at a whole multiple of it through an integer device pixel ratio. At those sizes every straight edge, every cut and the foot fall on whole pixels.

Every master at its native size

Each master rasterised by this browser at its native size, one canvas pixel per CSS pixel, then enlarged without smoothing on its pixel grid; then at 2x.

hero-large 128 at 2x (curved socket), 1:1
  • Native sizes only. 16, 20, 32, 48, the hero at 64, the hero-large masters at 128, 256 and 512, and 180 for the apple-touch icon. The component picks the master by size; give it a native size.
  • Arc. 0.25 of the grid in full ink at 128 px and up (1 px at 128, 2 at 256, 4 at 512), 1 px in the 180 master. At 64, 48, 32, 20 and 16 px exactly one device pixel of ink at 65 %, written as the solid #606062 (#646466 on white), so the needle's hidden top cannot show through it.
  • Hub and foot. Hub r 0.75: reveal 0.125 at 512 (2 px, curved), 0.125 at 256 and 0.25 at 128 (1 px, hinted to whole pixels). A hinted socket is right at 1x only, so on 2x screens the 128 draws the curved socket with reveal 0.25 and the 256 draws the 512 socket, both 2 device px. One colour and reverse keep 0.25: hinted at 128, curved at 256 (2 px) and 512 (4 px). Hub r 4 with a 1 px hinted reveal in the 180 master, shipped as a 180 px PNG. Pixel foot 3 x 3 px in the hero and the 48 master, 2 x 2 px at 32, 1 px at 20, the pixel above the wall end at 16.
  • Ring. Hero-large masters 0.25 at 50 % (1 px at 128); hero 0.5 at 35 % (1 px); 180 master 1 px at 50 %; 48 and 32 masters 1 px at 20 %; dropped at 20 and 16. Always whole pixels and always lighter than the arc.
  • Curves as polylines. 0.5° steps in the hero, the hero-large masters, mono, reverse and 180 master, 2° in the small masters and on the hub. Chrome flattens true SVG arcs coarsely enough to move a 1 px edge's coverage by tens of points.
  • Afterglow. One fill under a mask of stacked opaque grey sectors: 64 in the hero, the hero-large masters and the 180 master, 32 in the 48 master, 16, 10 and 6 in the small masters. Every pixel is written once, so 8 bit rounding cannot drift the colour.

In use

The header lockup in the app's 56 px bar on paper, with its real navigation. The 16 master as the favicon, on a paper square so it holds on light and dark tab strips. One colour and reverse at 128, the 180 master as the apple-touch icon (iOS applies its own mask).

Lockups

The mark's floor sits on the wordmark baseline. Wordmark Big Shoulders 800, sentence case, tracking 0 (+0.005em at 23 px in the header); descriptor Instrument Sans 500, muted, sentence case. Every letter is outlined in the files, so they render without the fonts.

Geometry, ready for React

Written by the generator to src/components/Logo.tsx.

// Turnout logo: the "Swing" mark and the wordmark. Generated by design/studio-a/swing-final.mjs (edit the generator,
// not this file; the design sheet is design/studio-a/swing-final.html).
// Mark: two walls on one floor, an arc from the right wall, a needle swung up from a round hub set into the end of the
// left wall ("pivot encastré"), the afterglow behind the needle.
// SIZE RULE. Each master is pixel-true at its native CSS size and at any integer devicePixelRatio: 16 (favicon),
// 20 (app header), 32, 48, 64 (hero), 128, 256 and 512 (the hero-large family, whose socket is cut for each size;
// one colour and reverse have their own 256 and 512).
// Mark picks the master for the box it is given (the largest native size not above it) and draws it at its native
// size, or the largest whole multiple that fits, centred on whole pixels: a 40 px box gets the 32 master with 4 px
// around it, never a 32 master stretched to 40. Give it a native size and the mark fills the box. It never shrinks in
// a flex row (flex: none). Its parent must put it on whole CSS pixels: a box centred against a fractional line height
// lands between pixels and blurs every edge.
// RETINA. At 128 and 256 the socket round the hub is a 1 px staircase hinted to the 1x pixel grid; doubled it would
// show 2 px nubs. Those masters carry ink2x, the same walls with the true curved socket, and a resolution media query
// draws it instead at 1.5 dppx and up (2x screens, and zoom of 150 % and more).
// Colours: ink = var(--mark-ink), #111114, never the surrounding text colour; needle and afterglow = var(--accent);
// below 128 px the outer arc is ink at 65 % over the ground as a solid colour: var(--mark-arc), #606062 on paper
// #F2F3F3; on a white board pass ground="white" for var(--mark-arc-white), #646466 (or let the board set
// --mark-arc: var(--mark-arc-white)). At 128 px and up the arc is full ink and 0.25 of the 32 grid.
// tone="mono" draws in currentColor; its arc below 128 px is currentColor at 65 % mixed over var(--mark-ground) (default
// the page colour; set it to the colour under the mark, e.g. #111114 for white on ink) into a solid colour.
// tone="reverse" is paper and raspberry on an ink ground. Every shape is a fill, painted in this order: afterglow, ring,
// needle, arc, ink.
// The afterglow mask's id is named after the afterglow it holds (turnout-glow-<glowId>), not per instance: ids from
// useId repeat across separately rendered server segments (after a client-side navigation two masks shared one id).
// Copies with the same id are identical, so any one of them renders correctly. Keep marks out of display: none
// subtrees where you can: a mask inside one does not paint in every browser.
import type { CSSProperties } from "react";

type Master = {
  vb: number;
  native: number;
  floor: number; // the wall bottom, in viewBox units: it sits on the wordmark baseline
  glowId: string; // the afterglow's name: masters with the same glowId have the same mask
  glow: { max: number; region: string; sectors: [d: string, grey: string][] };
  ring?: [d: string, opacity: number];
  needle: string;
  arc?: string; // the 65 % arc; absent when the arc is full ink (then it is part of ink)
  ink: string;
  ink2x?: string; // ink with the curved socket, drawn instead of ink at 1.5 dppx and up
};

// The afterglow of the hero-large family (the same needle, arc centreline and sectors at 128, 256 and 512, one colour too).

/** The native CSS sizes: draw the mark at one of these. */
export const SWING_SIZES = [16, 20, 32, 48, 64, 128, 256, 512] as const;
export type SwingSize = (typeof SWING_SIZES)[number];

const TONES = {
  color: { ink: "var(--mark-ink, #111114)", needle: "var(--accent, #E0115F)", k: 1 },
  mono: { ink: "currentColor", needle: "currentColor", k: 0.6 },
  reverse: { ink: "var(--paper, #F2F3F3)", needle: "var(--accent, #E0115F)", k: 1.25 }, // on an ink ground
} as const;
export type MarkTone = keyof typeof TONES;
export type MarkGround = "paper" | "white";

/** The 65 % arc below 128 px, as a solid colour over the ground. */
function arcFill(tone: MarkTone, ground: MarkGround): string {
  if (tone === "mono") return `color-mix(in srgb, currentColor 65%, var(--mark-ground, ${ground === "white" ? "#FFFFFF" : "var(--paper, #F2F3F3)"}))`;
  if (tone === "reverse") return "#A3A4A5"; // paper at 65 % over ink
  return ground === "white" ? "var(--mark-arc-white, #646466)" : "var(--mark-arc, #606062)";
}

/** The master drawn for a CSS size. One colour and reverse have their own 256 and 512 (reveal 0.25, curved). */
export function swingMaster(size: number, tone: MarkTone = "color"): Master {
  if (size >= 128) {
    if (tone !== "color") return size >= 512 ? SWING.heroLargeOne512 : size >= 256 ? SWING.heroLargeOne256 : SWING.heroLarge;
    return size >= 512 ? SWING.heroLarge512 : size >= 256 ? SWING.heroLarge256 : SWING.heroLarge;
  }
  return size >= 64 ? SWING.hero : size >= 48 ? SWING.px48 : size >= 32 ? SWING.px32 : size >= 20 ? SWING.px20 : SWING.px16;
}

// Draws ink2x instead of ink at 1.5 dppx and up. Global, but it only touches the two class names below.
const RETINA_CSS = ".turnout-ink-2x{display:none}@media (min-resolution:1.5dppx),(-webkit-min-device-pixel-ratio:1.5){.turnout-ink-1x{display:none}.turnout-ink-2x{display:inline}}";

export function Mark({ size = 32, tone = "color", ground = "paper", title, className, style }: { size?: number; tone?: MarkTone; ground?: MarkGround; title?: string; className?: string; style?: CSSProperties }) {
  const m = swingMaster(size, tone), t = TONES[tone], id = `turnout-glow-${m.glowId}`;
  // drawn size: the native size or its largest whole multiple in the box (below 16 px the 16 master is scaled down)
  const drawn = size < m.native ? size : m.native * Math.floor(size / m.native), off = Math.floor((size - drawn) / 2), u = m.vb / drawn;
  return (
    <svg width={size} height={size} viewBox={`${-off * u} ${-off * u} ${size * u} ${size * u}`} className={className} style={{ flex: "none", ...style }} role={title ? "img" : undefined} aria-label={title} aria-hidden={title ? undefined : true} focusable="false">
      <mask id={id} maskUnits="userSpaceOnUse" x="0" y="0" width={m.vb} height={m.vb}>
        {m.glow.sectors.map(([d, grey]) => <path key={d} d={d} fill={grey} />)}
      </mask>
      <path d={m.glow.region} fill={t.needle} fillOpacity={Math.min(0.9, m.glow.max * t.k)} mask={`url(#${id})`} />
      {m.ring && <path d={m.ring[0]} fill={t.ink} fillOpacity={m.ring[1]} />}
      <path d={m.needle} fill={t.needle} />
      {m.arc && <path d={m.arc} fill={arcFill(tone, ground)} />}
      {m.ink2x ? (
        <>
          <style>{RETINA_CSS}</style>
          <path className="turnout-ink-1x" d={m.ink} fill={t.ink} />
          <path className="turnout-ink-2x" d={m.ink2x} fill={t.ink} />
        </>
      ) : (
        <path d={m.ink} fill={t.ink} />
      )}
    </svg>
  );
}

// The mark that goes with a wordmark size: its height (arc top to floor) is 0.76 to 0.875 of the cap height.
const markFor = (fontSize: number) => fontSize <= 26 ? 20 : fontSize <= 40 ? 32 : fontSize <= 54 ? 48 : fontSize <= 100 ? 64 : 128;

/**
 * The lockup: mark, "Turnout" in Big Shoulders 800 (sentence case), and optionally the descriptor "crowd to campaign"
 * in Instrument Sans 500, muted, sentence case. The mark's floor sits on the text baseline.
 * size = the wordmark's font size in px: 23 in the app header (mark 20, gap 6, tracking +0.005em), 63 for the hero
 * (mark 64, gap 12). Tracking is 0 at every other size.
 */
export function Wordmark({ size = 20, tagline = false, mark = true, className }: { size?: number; tagline?: boolean; mark?: boolean; className?: string }) {
  const ms = markFor(size), m = swingMaster(ms), drop = ms - (m.floor * ms) / m.vb, gap = Math.max(6, Math.round(size * 0.19));
  return (
    <span className={`inline-flex items-baseline${className ? " " + className : ""}`} style={{ color: "var(--ink)", lineHeight: 1 }}>
      {mark && <Mark size={ms} style={{ position: "relative", top: drop, marginRight: gap }} />}
      <span style={{ fontFamily: "var(--font-display)", fontWeight: 800, fontSize: size, lineHeight: 1, fontOpticalSizing: "auto", letterSpacing: size === 23 ? "0.005em" : 0 }}>Turnout</span>
      {tagline && (
        <span className="hidden sm:inline" style={{ marginLeft: 10, fontFamily: "var(--font-sans)", fontWeight: 500, fontSize: 13, lineHeight: 1, color: "var(--muted)" }}>
          crowd to campaign
        </span>
      )}
    </span>
  );
}
